金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑 ， 但仍維持高檔 ， 顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

歐 
元 
區 
法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，服務業、製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐洲議會代表團於 10 月 27 日至 29 日訪問中國，料將討論稀土及綠能
技術的依賴、貿易失衡，以及歐洲企業在中國面臨的不平等待遇問題。 
⚫ 法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日
再度發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工。 
短評 
⚫ 法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革 ， 也難以快速推出安撫反對派
的妥協法案或者公共支出 。 部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延
續至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。 
⚫ 市場預期歐洲央行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場。 
日 
本 
貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月出口年增 16.7%。 
⚫ 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%。 
⚫ 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3。 
短評 
⚫ 出口維持強勁、服務需求亦持續擴張，惟中東局勢推升能源進口成本，
使貿易逆差擴大，並加重國內通膨壓力。隨成本上漲逐步由商品擴散至
服務價格，通膨黏著性可能進一步提高，為日本央行貨幣政策正常化提
供支撐；另一方面，原物料供應受限及投入成本居高不下，已開始影響
製造業動能與企業信心，後續仍須關注高成本環境對經濟成長的制約。 
中 
國 
第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%。 
⚫ 1-9 月累計零售額年增率持平為 1.1%。 
⚫ 1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2% 
⚫ 1-9 月房地產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%。 
⚫ 9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點至 5.5%；累計 1-9 月工業
生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐盟計劃最快將於 2027 年首季擴大對中國產品的進口限制，涵蓋化學
品、家具及機械等產品，不再僅限電動車。 
⚫ 中國銀行成功發行市場首筆掛鉤 DR001 基準利率的「三農」專項金融

4

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index) 初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%， 長期通膨預期亦由3.4%升至 3.5%， 雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示 ， 此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔 ， 並維護國內產業利益 。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯 (Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免 ， 外媒亦將此次調整與11 月 3 日期中選舉連結 。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加 ， 使民眾傾向保守消費 。 若以產業區分 ， 歐元區服務業 、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大 ， 各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度

善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁 ， 受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動 ， 出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品 ， 進一步擴散至房屋維護 、 公共運輸 、 住宿及外食等服務項目 ，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大 ， 亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐 。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%， 其中製造業 、 批發零售業年增率分別達5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能 ， 抵銷建築業年減4.4%等拖累因素影響 。 整體而言 ， 第三季GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致 ；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來 ， 儘管政府10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分 ， 對香港出口激增 121.1%、 對台灣 、 韓國出口增幅達96%， 對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定， 支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行 （BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息 。 央行指出 ， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，

源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖

持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出

申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧 、 高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展 ，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出 ，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

2026/10/12 收盤價： 527.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 
611.7%，毛利率 85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利

億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營收成

百分比，而季減中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分
比。 
➢ 展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，

88.1%，EPS為 30.35 元。

DRAM產能持續滿載，預期 DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產
品將持續供不應求。1C 產品預期於 27Q1 開始驗證，有
利打入 AI伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此
兆豐預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，

營 運 分 析

南亞科技為全球前四大 DRAM廠商 
南亞科技主要致力 DRAM (動態隨機存取記憶體)的研發、設計、製造與銷售，
開發自主技術，發展智慧財產權，培育關鍵技術研發團隊，落實綠色科技及
智慧製造全面提升製程效能與產能，是全球第4大的 DRAM公司。公司已成
功開發 10 奈米級 DRAM 製程技術，將可微縮至少 3 個世代，並用以發展
DDR5及 LPDDR5 等下世代產品，強化公司自主技術開發與製程轉換佈局。
第二世代 10 奈米級製程技術(1B)產品 16Gb DDR5 已完成設計與試產，於
2024 年量產。南亞科技具備完整 DRAM 產品線，可因應智慧世代的發展，
滿足多元化的產品需求。預期 1C產品於 27Q1 開始驗證。

26Q3 ASP成長帶動營收毛利雙雙上升 
26Q3 營收  1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 611.7%，毛利率
85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利益為 1068.9 億元，業外收入 24.38
億，稅後淨利為 876.3 億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營
收成長及毛利雙雙成長受惠平均銷售單價季增超過六十位數百分比，而季減
中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分比。

26Q4合約價持續上漲  
展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，且南亞科 AI 相關產
品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價
將持續於 26Q4 上漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 88.1%，EPS 為 30.35
元。

圖三、DRAM合約價報價走勢圖

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

南亞科完成 787.2 億元私募案，攜手四大客戶搶佔 AI 供應鏈先機 
南亞科日前完成總額新台幣  787.2 億元的私募認購，吸引四大核心客戶參
與。增資後，相關投資方持股比例達 10.19%。雙方同時簽署供應協議，確
保未來能穩定供應多元 DRAM 產品組合。主要目的在於強化南亞科與客戶
的戰略合作，透過 e-SSD 與網通產品等領域，建立 AI 及雲端服務供應商
（CSP）供應鏈中的雙贏局面。私募資金將注資新廠以提升產能，進一步確
立南亞科在 AI 價值鏈中的市場地位。本次私募後，南亞科總發行股數將達 
34.5 億股。

記憶體供不應求，產能持續擴充 
目前產能為 6.5 萬片/月，產能利用率維持滿載，2026Q1~Q3 資本支出 276
億，2026 以不超過 697 億元為上限，設備佔比五成．5A新廠第一階段 35,900
片/月 於 2026~2029 資本支出約 3,466 億．新廠預計於 2027 年初開始裝機，
主要生產 1B/1C/1D製程，預期 27年底開出 20k/月，2028 年達每月投片量
達 30k/月，主要因應網通、eSSD等產品之強勁需求。

AI 帶動記憶體強勁需求，有利報價持續走升 
展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於 DRAM 產能持續滿
載，預期DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產品將持續供不應求。1C產品預期於 27Q1
開始驗證，有利打入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此兆豐
預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，毛利率83.3/86.3%，EPS
為 77.85/133.2 元。

記憶體持續供不應
求，報價持續走升。 
2027 新產能主要 用
於生產 1B/1C/1D 製
程。 
大型私募案確保未來
穩定供應多元 DRAM
產品組合。

研 究  報 告                
南亞科(2408.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
價格與⻑約重議價推升獲利，AI 需求⽀撐中⻑期成⻑  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠與記憶體供不應求持續，目前⻑約覆蓋率約50%～70%之間，合約期限從
季度到⻑達3年不等，其中，3年期合約佔總產出20%以上，有助營運能見度提
升；另隨著AI應用占比提升，有助降低記憶體景氣循環影響。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整，ASP季增超過60%： 3Q26營收季增
61.9%至1,336.5億元，年增611.7%，受惠平均售價季增60+%，其⻑約貢獻超
過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率79.5%攀升至85.7%，
營業淨利率80%，季增6.3ppts，營業費用75.88億元，費用增加主因當期及未
來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利876.27億元，稅後EPS 25.4元，優於預期。 
4Q26供不應求延續，預期ASP持續上揚、出貨持平：展望4Q26，公司預期市
場供不應求持續，ASP持續向上，惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，
預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，待4Q27新廠開始投產，預估
4Q26營收季增21.7%至1,626.7億元，⽑利率86.6%，稅後31.29元。 
随新廠產能開出，目標AI基礎設施逐步提升：新廠規劃1Q27裝機，因無大
幅增加產出的空間，加上庫存處於低水位，預期2027年初出貨量持平。南
亞科目前AI基礎設施相關營收占比已超過20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統管
理、eSSD以及RDIMM模組等，2030年以前目標提升至40～50%。 
 投資風險 
AI基礎建設投資放緩、客製化AI/WoW產品進度不如預期

### Page 2

2 
| 營運概況  
3Q26 反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整 ， ASP 季增超過 60% ：3Q26 營收季增 61.9%至 1,336.5 億元 ， 年增 611.7% ，
受惠平均售價季增 60+%，其⻑約貢獻超過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率 79%攀升至 85.7%，
營業淨利 1,068.9 億元，營業淨利率 80%，季增 6.3ppts，營業費用 75.88 億元，費用增加主因當期及未來的員
工薪酬增加 ， 稅後淨利 876.27 億元 ， 淨利率 65.6% ， 稅後 EPS 25.4 元 ， 優於預期 。 產品結構部份 ， DDR4/LPDDR4
佔比逾 60%，DDR5 約佔 10%；此外，AI 基礎設施(含伺服器、網路、儲存及系統管理)占營收超過 20%。 
4Q26 供不應求延續，預期 ASP 持續上揚、出貨持平：展望 4Q26，公司預期市場供不應求持續，ASP 持續向上，
惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，直到 4Q27 新廠開始投
產，預估 4Q26 營收季增 21.7%至 1,626.7 億元，⽑利率 86.6%，稅後 31.29 元。此外，就供需市況部份，隨著 AI
運算主導 DRAM 需求成⻑ ， 延伸至網路 、 儲存及系統管理等應用 ， 推升高容量 、 高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求 ，
而傳統應用因受限於供給不足加上報價調漲，轉而降低規格、延⻑⽣命週期來因應；另供給則因新廠建置、設備
交期、製程導入，均需要較⻑的前置時間，預期供不應求狀況在 2027 年至 2028 年前難以解決。 
随新廠產能開出，目標 AI 基礎設施應用占比逐步提升：產能規劃方面，新廠規劃 1Q27 裝機，因無大幅增加產出
的空間，再加上庫存消耗後，正處低水位，預期 2027 年初出貨量持平，隨著 4Q27 新廠開始投產後有望逐步提
升，目標 2028 年達每月投片量３萬片，2030 年年底達到每月投片量５萬片。此外，第三代 10 奈米級製程(1C)
預計 1Q27 開始客戶驗證，並同步開發第四代(1D)、第五代(1E)製程及 EUV，均按計畫推進。另規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，預計 2028 年年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)、晶圓堆疊等 3D IC 先進技術，未來擴建
皆以客戶⻑期合約的 AI 專案為目標。南亞科目前 AI 基礎設施相關營收占比已超過 20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統
管理、eSSD 以及 RDIMM 模組等，2030 年以前目標提升至 40～50%；此外，目前⻑約覆蓋率約 50%～70%之
間，合約期限從季度到⻑達 3 年不等，其中，3 年期合約佔總產出 20%以上，有助營運能見度提升；另與客戶合
作 AI 相關晶圓對晶圓技術 ASIC 專案，應用範圍涵蓋雲端 AI 運算、邊緣運算及物理 AI，已有營收貢獻，雖然目
前規模不大，後續業務發展值得關注。預估 2027 年營收 7,378.76 億元，⽑利率 87.1%，稅後 EPS 141.9 元，維
持「買進」之投資建議，目標價 710 元(3.8 倍 PBR*2027BVPS(F))。

### Page 1

Poya  (5904.TWO)
Strong 3Q as Expected; Tougher Comparisons Ahead
CITI'S TAKE 
Poya’s 3Q26 net profit +26% y/y came largely in-line with our forecast, and 
5% ahead of BBG consensus. SSSG of 7.0%, higher GPM, opex savings and 
stronger op leverage lifted OPM to 17.5%, the key drivers to strong 
profitability. Looking into 4Q, tougher comp should moderate sales and 
earnings growth despite fast store additions and solid operation execution. 
We maintain Neutral as the strong share price rally YTD appears to reflect 
most positives from robust SSSG and margin expansion. A higher comp 
base and potentially intensifying competition limit the room for further re-
rating. 
3Q26 broadly in-line; solid margins boosted by strong op leverage — Poya 
reported 3Q26 net profits of NT$1.0bn +26% y/y, largely in-line with our est and 5% 
above BBG’s est. 3Q sales of NT$7.4bn +15% y/y were supported by solid SSSG 
+7.0% y/y with ticket size +4.9% and traffic +2.0%. Higher GPM, strong opex 
savings and op leverage lifted OPM to 17.5%, topping our/BBG est of 17.1%/16.6%. 
T otal store count reached 495 at end-3Q26 (+25 net increase in 9M26). 
4Q outlook: faster store expansion meets a higher comp base — We expect 30 net 
store additions in 4Q26E vs. 35 stores prior, bringing the year-end store count to 
525 and supporting continued sales growth. Demand for beauty-related products 
remains healthy. However, the elevated comparison base from 4Q26E onward 
should moderate SSSG and overall sales and earnings growth, despite the 
contribution from new stores. We believe this potential growth deceleration could 
temper investor enthusiasm and trigger profit-taking pressure.
Implications: Slightly positive reaction expected; maintain Neutral — We keep our 
earnings estimates for 26-28E largely unchanged in view of the in-line 3Q results. 
We expect a slightly positive market reaction to the results given the healthy margin 
expansion. However, strong sales had likely raised expectations for 3Q profitability, 
limiting the scope for a more meaningful positive surprise. Our Neutral thesis 
remains unchanged – we believe the strong share price rally has priced in much of 
the benefit from robust SSSG and margin improvement. Looking ahead, tougher 
comp could constrain growth, while potentially intensifying competition, including 
Cosmed’s introduction of Olive Young products, could challenge Poya’s beauty-led 
momentum. We therefore see limited room for further re-rating despite solid 
operating execution.

### Page 5

Poya  (5904.TWO)
27 October 2026 Citi Research
5
Poya
Company description
Poya is the largest retailer of fast-moving consumer goods in Taiwan, focusing
on low-priced cosmetics, accessories, clothing and food products for females
aged 15-49. Poya had 470 stores in Taiwan at the end of 2025 and believes
that the market can accommodate up to 700-800 stores before reaching
saturation.
Investment strategy
We have a Neutral rating on Poya. We think YTD rally has baked in most

positives, including strong SSSG and solid margin expansion. While
fundamentals remain solid, tougher comparisons from 4Q26E could
moderate this year's 6-7% SSSG, lowering operating leverage and capping
further earnings upside. Meanwhile, Cosmed's expanding rollout of Olive
Young brands is intensifying competition for beauty spending. Although
Poya's SSSG has remained resilient, braoder distribution of these brands
could divert customer traffic and increase promotional pressure. We continue

to recognize Poya's execution in store expansion and ongoing efficiency
improvements. However, with these strengths largely reflected in the share
price and growth momentum facing a higher hurdle from 4Q26E onward, we
see a more balanced risk/reward.
Valuation
We derive our 12-month target price of NT$70 for Poya from a DCF valuation
method. Our assumptions include a terminal growth rate of 4.5% and WACC
of 10.1% (beta of 1.05; risk-free rate of 4%; risk premium of 7.0%; cost of equity

of 11.3%). Our target price implies a P/E of 18x on our NTM EPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: intense price competition among online retailers, slower store traffic,
margin dilution due to aggressive store expansion, and a soft macro
environment.
Key upside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: easing price competition among online retailers, stronger store

traffic, margin expansion thanks to strong cost control, and a stronger macro
environment.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

Document 128: 261027_daiwa_faraday

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_faraday.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Faraday released a mixed set of results and guidance on 27 
October. 3Q26 core results missed consensus on an unfavourable product 
mix and higher opex, while 4Q26 guidance was broadly intact on delayed 
MCU business rebalancing. Regardless of the near-term hiccup, 
management expects its 3 product lines of mass-production (MP), IP and 
non-recurrent engineering (NRE) to fire on all cylinders into 2027 for a 
double-digit % YoY revenue growth. This concurs with our turnaround story 
for the counter, and we reiterate our Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD220.

What’s the impact: Results mixed, guidance intact. Faraday’s 3Q26 net 
profit came in at TWD279m, 9% above our estimate and beating 
consensus by a meaningful 38%. Despite the beat, dynamics were mixed 
(table on p.2). Core operations missed despite a higher top line, which was 
spurred by MP on stronger-than-expected MCU sales from its 50%-owned 
Artery (6907 TT, not rated) for a lower gross margin (GM) due to an 
unfavourable revenue mix as MP carries a lower GM than the other 2 lines. 
Higher opex, as a result of salary hikes and employee bonuses, further 
pressured core operations with operating profit missing our/consensus 
estimates by 36%/11%, but non-operating gains from marked-to-market 
investments more than offset the miss. Guidance calls for a roughly flat 
4Q26 top line with a 45% GM, broadly in line with our expectation.

Firing on all cylinders across all 3 lines. In our previous note, we 
expected rebalancing in the MCU business due to demand pull-in on price 
hikes and supply chain uncertainties. It failed to happen in 3Q26 thanks to 
early pull-in of OSAT capacity support, thus pushing back the rebalancing 
to 4Q26 — a key reason why top line was guided to be flat despite the 
other 2 lines of IP and NRE growing QoQ. That said, management expects 
the MCU business to return to a growth track in 2027 after the near-term 
hiccup. Its delayed advanced packaging (AP) ramp on design complexity 
and supply constraint looks set to accelerate in 2027 on OSAT capacity 
improvement which, together with a milestone of 2nm/GAA design-win for a 
cloud ASIC, should add to upside for the NRE and MP business next year. 
All in, we lower our 2026-27E EPS by 9-13% to factor in the higher opex.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we keep our 12M 
TP for Faraday unchanged at TWD220 as we maintain our 4-quarter 
forward PER target of 37x. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with key 
downside risks of silicon inflation and geopolitical overhangs leading to 
global inflation and prolonging a non-AI recovery.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 6-9% below the consensus 
forecasts likely on the street’s lower opex assumptions.

27 October 2026

Turnaround confirmed (II)

➢ 3Q26 results a mixed bag; 4Q26 guidance broadly intact 
➢ Firing on all cylinders into 2027 after near-term hiccups 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) call and 12M TP of TWD220

Company profile 
Founded in 1993, Faraday is a hybrid design service provider offering IP and ASIC design services to 
fabless, foundry and system houses. It has R&D and marketing locations in the US, Japan, China, India, 
and Vietnam.

### Page 5

5

Faraday Technology (3035 TT): 27 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 10 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Faraday’s Board of Directors (BoD) consists of 10 members, including 4 independent directors,
representing 40% of the board—significantly above the regulatory minimum of 20%. Three of the
10 directors are female, reflecting progress toward gender diversity at the board level. The roles
of Chairman and President are separated, supporting stronger governance checks and
balances. However, most non independent directors concurrently serve as executive managers,
which may elevate concerns regarding board independence and management oversight from an
ESG governance perspective.
Capital management 1
Over the past 10 years, Faraday has kept its dividend payout ratio at above 70% most of the
time, in spite of an up or down cycle. As an IC design service company without capex for
manufacturing fabs, Faraday's high dividend payout is appropriate, in our view.
Related party &
transaction 1
UMC, which is Faraday's largest shareholder, is Faraday's major foundry for its turnkey
business. For IC design service companies, a close relationship with foundries is essential since
their design should build on a foundry's process node and their turnkey businesses also require
a foundry's capacity support. UMC's support offers Faraday a competitive advantage, in our
view. Faraday has developed many standard cell and I/O libraries for UMC, covering logic and
mixed-signal specialty processes across 0.5μ to 22nm.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Faraday has set several evaluation standards for its existing and new suppliers and it requires all
suppliers to get ISO9001 and ISO14001 certifications for quality and environmental
management. In 2024, 100% of its wafer, packaging and testing providers had ISO9001 and
ISO14001 certifications. In addition, Faraday also has on-site audits (quality system and process
quality audit, CSR evaluation and environment management system) for its suppliers with a mid-
to-long term target of conducting annual onsite audits for 100% of its suppliers.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 1
As an IC design service company, Faraday outsources its manufacturing and thus has no own
production sites. As such, Faraday's water use is primarily for general operations, not for
production. Faraday continues to conduct several water reduction measures to manage water
usage.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
Faraday has established a green supply chain in accordance with the EU Directive on the
Restriction of the Use of Certain Hazardous Substances in Electrical and Electronic Equipment
(RoHS) and with the EU Chemicals Policy (REACH).
Faraday has a mid-to-long term target of carefully evaluating and selecting raw materials with
low hazard levels to comply with the Hazardous Substance-Free Product Standard. All of its
packaging materials are 100% lead-free and halogen-free.
E GHG emissions 1
With Faraday's energy saving measures, it reduced the GHG emissions intensity gradually. As
an IC design service company without onsite production, Faraday's GHG emissions are much
lower than other semiconductor manufacturing companies. Faraday's low power design also
helps reduce GHG emissions. In 2019, Faraday completed the development of its ultra-low
power consumption 22nm IP solution.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

研 究  報 告                
力積電(6770.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
記憶體漲價貢獻 4Q26 獲利維持高檔，規劃 3D AI Foundry 新廠  
 投資評價與建議 
3Q26獲利優於預期，且4Q26毛利率有望重回50%以上之高峰，加上明年記憶
體、成熟製程市況展望正向，故我們給予2.3倍PB，持續看好後續營運動能。 
 重點評論分析 
看好4Q26毛利率重回過往50%以上高峰： 基於 ： (1)IPC 、 網通和醫療等應用支
撐力積電DRAM代工需求，Flash業務中，SLC NAND則因記憶體大廠先進製
程產能排擠，獲得車用、網通等小容量應用降規需求挹注；(2) AI伺服器持續
支撐PMIC、分離式元件需求，另考量中介層與Wafer on Wafer於新竹廠區陸
續復線，將有利於3D AI Foundry營收動能回溫。預估有望季增18.3%至252.4
億元，毛利率53.6%，稅後EPS 1.86元。  
正向看待明年展望： 展望明年 ， 我們認為 ： (1)AI需求維持強勁且應用持續擴大 ，
預期記憶體供不應求之情況將至少延續至 2027年，其中市場預估明年DRAM
報價將延續漲勢；(2)邏輯業務中，PMIC、分離式元件等8吋產能持續受惠AI強
勁需求，我們預期明年台系成熟製程廠商產能利用率有望達90%以上，公司規
劃明年漲價約10~20%且產能將以LTA客戶為優先；(3)考量目前無塵室空間幾
乎用罄，公司將開始評估新廠並著重3D AI Foundry產能擴產，但計畫透過設
備租賃、客戶給付預付款形式由客戶參與部分投資金額，以此控制公司資本支
出。我們初步預估明年營收達1,241.8億元，年增60.3%，稅後EPS 10.65元。 
 投資風險 
記憶體報價下滑、3D AI Foundry進展不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
3Q26獲利優於預期 ：力積電公告3Q26自結營收213.4億元 ， 季增23.4% 、 年增80.3% ， 毛利率47.6% ， 稅後EPS 1.38
元，獲利表現優於預期，因毛利率受惠於平均ASP季增24%、產能利用率由2Q26的87%提升至3Q26的97%。 
4Q26營收雙位數成⻑，看好毛利率重回過往50%以上高峰：展望4Q26，基於：(1)記憶體大廠優先投資HBM並逐
步退出利基型DRAM市場，加上IPC、網通和醫療等應用支撐，使得力積電DRAM代工需求維持穩健，至於與美光
合作之1P製程進展順利，預計2Q27開始試產，Flash業務中，SLC NAND則因記憶體大廠先進製程產能排擠，獲得
車用、網通等小容量應用降規需求挹注，另公司維持MLC NAND今年底tape out之進度；(2)邏輯業務中，儘管消
費性電子需求不佳，但AI伺服器需求強勁持續支撐PMIC、分離式元件需求，其中公司PMIC代工服務已從0.15微米
切入55奈米並開始出貨，且獲客戶三年綁約，反映需求強勁，另考量中介層與Wafer on Wafer於新竹廠區陸續復
線，將有利於4Q26 AI Foundry營收動能回溫。綜上所述，公司估本季營收有望雙位數季增，我們則預估有望季增
18.3%至252.4億元，毛利率53.6%，回到過往50%以上之高峰水準，稅後EPS 1.86元。  
正向看待明年展望：展望明年，我們認為：(1)AI需求維持強勁且應用持續擴大，預期記憶體大廠優先投資先進製程
之策略不變，故認為整體記憶體市場供不應求之情況將至少延續至2027年，其中市場預估明年DRAM報價將延續
漲勢(但漲幅收斂)，隱含公司代工價格具上漲動能；(2)邏輯業務中，半導體庫存維持健康，且PMIC、分離式元件等
8吋產能持續受惠AI強勁需求(其中分離式元件80%產能已簽訂LTA) ， 我們預期明年台系成熟製程廠商產能利用率有
望達90%以上，基於產能吃緊將有利於明年成熟製程廠商議價能力，公司規劃明年漲價約10~20%且產能將以LTA
客戶為優先；(3)3D AI Foundry業務仍為未來投資主軸，且產能規劃與進展大致如期，唯⼀有較新進展之業務則為
Wafer on Wafer規劃1H27量產 ， 另公司考量目前無塵室空間幾乎用罄 ， 公司將開始評估新廠並著重3D AI Foundry
產能擴產，但計畫透過設備租賃、客戶給付預付款形式由客戶參與30~60%的投資金額，以此控制公司資本支出。
我們初步預估明年營收達1,241.8億元，年增60.3%，稅後EPS 10.65元。

百和(9938) 
建議：維持買進 
結論：最壞已過且股價低於淨值，靜待基本面起飛

一、3Q26 自結 EPS 0.62 元，低於預期；主要受到網
布毛利率下滑且業外利息費用所致 
百和 3Q26 營收 44.09 億元(+15.9% QoQ / +5.8% YOY)，
就各產品比重來看，黏扣帶 12.3%、織鞋帶 44.1%、鬆
緊帶 13%、射出勾 6.6%、針織經編提花網布 20.1%以
及其他 3.9%。3Q26 就各產品線來看，所有產品線均成
長，僅有經編網布衰退，其中鬆緊帶+16%YoY、黏扣帶
+9.7%YoY、織鞋帶+9% YOY、射出勾+6.7%YoY 以及
針織經編提花網布-8.4% YOY。獲利方面，3Q26 毛利
率 29.5%，與 2Q26 約略持平，也較3Q25 下滑 6.3%，
主要反映關稅影響、產品組合以及受到美伊戰爭影響原
物料成本上揚所致；就 3Q26 各產品線毛利率來看，粘
扣帶 27.6%、 鬆緊帶37.9%、 射出勾44%、 織鞋帶30.4%、
經編網布 17.2％以及其他 37.5%。其中經編網布毛利率
較 2Q26 回升 2.7%，但仍低於 2025 年水準，主要反映
產品組合差異以及稼動率的影響；3Q26 合併營業利益
達 4.35 億元，營業利益率 9.9%，業外淨損 1.41 億元，
主要反映百和-KY 利息費用，稅前淨利 2.94 億元，稅後
歸屬母公司淨利 1.84 億元，EPS 0.62 元。  
二 、 考量原物料成本提升且通膨持續影響全球消費景氣 ，
下修百和 2026 年營收為低個位數增長且因毛利率表現
低於預期，下修 2026 年 EPS 為 2.48 元 
9M26 前十大客戶佔比 65.5%，德國品牌仍為第一大客
戶 ， 佔比25.1%，YOY-5%、 美系品牌15.7%，YOY+2%，
其中鞋子持平、服飾類+11%、瑞士品牌為第三大客戶，
佔營收比重 6.1%，YOY-11%，New Balance 占比 4.4%，
YOY-11%。HOKA 已為前十大客戶 ， 佔營收比重2.3%，
YOY+58%，為前九月成長性最佳客戶。就產品應用別
來看 ，前九月Footwear 占比 60.4%、Apparel 25.3%、
3C 2.8%； 以前九個月YOY 來看 ，Footwear-1% YOY、
Apparel +12% YOY、3C 產品+16% YOY 以及醫療-3% 
YOY，但單就第三季表現來看，Footwear 持平、
Apparel+27% YOY、3C+20% YOY 以及醫療+31% 
YOY，顯示其他應用端需求起來，未來百和將持續著重
其他類別發展，不單僅限於鞋子跟衣服，醫療跟 3C 已
漸有成效。過去 3C 是客人指定材料，百和僅有加工；
但現在幾乎 80%都是百和的材料進行加工，做成客戶想
要的東西，附加價值較高。3C 及醫療目前營收占比約
6%，未來可望緩步增加。 
考量美伊戰爭引發原物料成本提升且戰爭仍未平息影響
全球消費景氣，輔以客戶下單仍顯保守；我們預估百和
2026 年營收為 161.25 億元 (+2.2% YoY)，其中傳產中
個位數成長、網布中~高個位數衰退；毛利率方面，考量
原物料價格上揚因素未解、關稅影響持續且經編網布毛
利率低於預期 ， 保守預估2026 年集團毛利率為 30.7%，
較 2025 年下滑 4.6%，下修本業獲利為 17.68 億元，營
業利益率 11%，業外利息費用與 2025 年大致相當，預
估稅後歸屬母公司淨利潤 7.41 億元 ， 下修EPS 為 2.48
元。 
資本支出方面，2025 年實際資本支出 19.01 億元，其中
廠房建物 26％、設備購置 72%以及其他 2%。9M26 資
本支出 22.69 億元，其中台灣百和3.31 億元、百和興業
19.38 億元；2026 年預期資本支出約 28.02 億元，主要
以百和興業為主，約占 3/4；台灣百和約 1/4，主要用在
機器設備。 
三、下修經編緹花網布 2026 年至中~高個位數衰退，長
期仍可望有轉單效益 
2025 年隨著品牌採用意願提升，網布營收貢獻達 34.08
億元，YOY+38%，毛利率 34.09%；2026 年原預估在
新產能以及核心客戶、新興客戶放量且打入成衣供應鏈
帶動，成長性不亞於 2025 年，但受到全球大環境不確
定性高且受到產品組合以及主要客戶新一代跑鞋主料轉
用梭織且非由百和-KY 供應影響，前九個月網布營收衰
4.9%，我們下修 2026 年經編緹花網布營收為 31.46 億
元，-7.68% YOY， 毛利率受到稼動率偏低且產品組合影
響，前三季僅 19.9%。現在網布往圓編、橫編等更多織
法開發，未來可望持續擴大應用面，未來隨著應用面擴
大且稼動率提升，毛利率有機會往 2025 年水準邁進。 
網布客戶占比來看 ，2025 年德系品牌仍是第一大客戶，

四、維持買進理由 
2026 年受到美伊戰爭引發原物料成本提升且戰爭仍未
平息影響全球消費景氣，輔以客戶下單仍顯保守；我們
預估百和 2026 年營收為 161.25 億元 (+2.2% YoY)，其
中傳產中個位數成長 、 網布中~高個位數衰退 ， 下修EPS
為 2.48 元。展望 2027 年，隨著品牌對應庫存調整到較
健康的水位，下單可望回到正常模式。大環境穩定後，
原物料成本可以趨於正常、關稅議題減緩，2027 年可望
優於 2026 年，預估 2027 年 EPS 3.23 元。股價方面，
考量最壞已過 、2027 年可望恢復成長且目前股價低於淨
值，故維持買進評等，目標價 48 元(15X 2027PER)。

Document 241: Anthropic IPO将至，澳洲最大银行坐拥15亿澳元浮盈

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic IPO将至，澳洲最大银行坐拥15亿澳元浮盈.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

澳⼤利亚联邦银⾏以约 1.07 亿美元低调⼊股 Anthropic E 轮融资，短短⼀年账⾯价值从 1.53
亿澳元飙升⾄ 15.3 亿澳元，增幅近⼗倍。若 Anthropic 以 2 万亿美元估值 IPO ，持股或进⼀
步攀升⾄ 32 亿澳元。更值得关注的是，该⾏并⾮单纯财务投资者 —— 双⽅已深度合作落地
反欺诈 AI 应⽤，并共建⼯程师培训项⽬。
摘要：
随着 Anthropic 上市进程临近，澳⼤利亚联邦银⾏（ CBA ）正站在⼀笔潜在巨额收益的⻔⼝。
CBA 持有的 Anthropic 股权账⾯价值已从最初的 1.53 亿澳元飙升⾄ 15.3 亿澳元，⼀年内增⻓近⼗
倍。据彭博测算，若 Anthropic 最终以 2 万亿美元估值完成上市，CBA 所持股份届时价值或将进
⼀步攀升⾄约 32 亿澳元，使这家澳洲最⼤银⾏成为此次 AI 独⻆兽 IPO 中罕⻅的银⾏业赢家。
这⼀浮盈源于 CBA 在 2025 年 3 ⽉参与 Anthropic 的 E 轮融资。彼时⼊股成本约合 1.07 亿美元。此

后， Anthropic 于今年 5 ⽉完成新⼀轮融资，最新估值升⾄ 9650 亿美元，推动 CBA 持股账⾯价值
⼤幅重估。值得注意的是，上述彭博测算为概算，未计⼊ IPO 可能带来的股权稀释效应，亦未反
映 CBA ⾃今年 8 ⽉年报发布以来持股情况的任何潜在变动。
CBA 与 Anthropic 的关系不⽌于财务投资。双⽅已建⽴业务合作伙伴关系，共同推进 AI 在银⾏业
务中的落地应⽤，涵盖反欺诈与反诈骗场景。 CBA 本⽉还宣布，旗下部分软件⼯程师将参与
Anthropic 专为提升 AI 技能⽽设计的新项⽬。
⼀笔低调的早期押注
CBA 于 2025 年 3 ⽉参与 Anthropic E 轮融资，⼊股时披露的股权价值约为 1.53 亿澳元（约合 1.075
亿美元）。这⼀时间节点早于 Anthropic 估值的⼤幅跃升，使 CBA 得以在相对较低的估值⽔平建⽴
头⼨。
截⾄去年 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇，该持股价值维持在⼊股时的披露⽔平。然⽽，今年 5 ⽉ Anthropic 完成新⼀轮

融资后，公司估值升⾄9650 亿美元， CBA 随即在今年 6 ⽉底的账⾯重估中将该持股价值调整⾄
15.3 亿澳元，⼀年内账⾯增值约⼗倍。
据彭博测算，若 Anthropic 以 2 万亿美元估值挂牌上市， CBA 所持股份届时价值约为 32 亿澳元，
较当前账⾯价值再度翻倍有余。
需要指出的是，上述测算存在若⼲不确定因素：
尽管如此，上述数字已⾜以说明， CBA 在这笔 AI 投资上的回报潜⼒在全球银⾏业中颇为突出。
彭博数据显⽰，在已知参与 Anthropic 融资的机构投资者中，银⾏类股东并不多⻅—— 今年 2
⽉， Anthropic 曾公开披露摩根⼤通参与了其 G 轮融资，⽽ CBA 的 E 轮持股则使其成为少数在
Anthropic 股权上有直接敞⼝的银⾏之⼀。
其⼀， IPO 通常伴随新股发⾏，将对现有股东持股⽐例产⽣稀释效应，⽽测算未将此纳⼊考量；
其⼆， CBA ⾃今年 8 ⽉年报发布后是否对持股进⾏过调整，⽬前尚⽆公开披露；其三， Anthropic
最终 IPO 估值仍存在较⼤变数。

Document 250: Anthropic招股書裡最值得看的14個細節

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic招股書裡最值得看的14個細節.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
Anthropic 招股書裡最值得看的 14 個細節 
2026/10/27

摘要： 
⚫ 華爾街見聞整理了人工智慧獨角獸 Anthropic 提交 S-1 招股書的核心數據與關鍵細節。公司
擬以代碼「ANT」登陸那斯達克，目標估值介於 1.5 兆至 2 兆美元之間。 
⚫ 營收五個季度漲了約 30 倍，第二季已經轉盈，上半年帳面卻虧了 807 億美元，算力帳單還簽
到了 2045 年……Anthropic 招股書裡最值得看的 14 個細節，不少都帶著這樣的反差。更少見
的是控制權：普通股東幾乎沒有話語權，50.1%的投票權握在七位創辦人手中。 
1. 瘋狂的營收增速與放緩跡象  
⚫ 營收從 2023 年的 4,100 萬美元飆升至 2025 年的 45.85 億美元，2026 上半年達 
162.86 億美元；第二季單季營收 115.55 億美元，五個季度內暴增約 30 倍。今年前 9 
個月累計收入突破 330 億美元，過去一年人均創收達 660 萬美元。不過第二季至第三季
營收季增幅已放緩至接近 50%，公司警示早期爆發增長後未來可能停滯。

### Page 2

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
2. 半年帳面虧損 807 億美元，主因為公允價值變動  
⚫ 2026 上半年帳面淨虧損達 807.48 億美元，但其中約 723.88 億美元是因公司估值大幅
上升，導致可轉換票據、衍生負債與 SAFE 評估價值提高所認列的帳面虧損，並無現金流
出。扣除股權激勵等非現金費用後，上半年調整後營業利潤為 16.18 億美元，調整後淨利
潤達 26.71 億美元。 
3. 算力成本佔比驟降，經營利潤成功轉正  
⚫ 每 1 美元營收對應的算力與基礎設施支出，從 2025 年第一季的超過 2.5 美元降至 
2026 年第二季的不足 50 美分（算力支出佔比由 2023 年的 10 倍以上降至 2026 上
半年的 55%）。2026 年第二季經營利潤達 9.74 億美元（利潤率 8%），上半年營運現
金流淨流入 39 億美元，主要受惠於「閉環研發」模型訓練效率提升、推理優化、跨晶片
自建調度及降價策略。

4. 客戶結構多元化，頭部集中度下降  
⚫ 今年前 9 個月前五大客戶貢獻 25% 營收，但佔比從第一季的約 36% 降至第三季的 
21%，已無單一客戶佔比超過 10%。年消費破 10 萬美元的客戶超過 11,900 家，超過
半數名列財富 500 強（但其支出佔自身總預算不到 0.1%）。淨收入留存率（不含訂閱）
高達約 900%，且第三季有超過 80% 的營收來自近 6 個月內發布的新模型。

### Page 3

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。

5. 5%至 7%的收入來自違規客戶  
⚫ 前 9 個月約有 5% 至 7%（約 16 億至 23 億美元）的營收來自事後被判定違反使用規
範或商業條款的客戶，主因是監控、調查至封號存在時間差。隨著 8、9 月加大封禁力
度，此類收入佔比將下降，可能對未來的營收與增速造成一定拉低。 
6. 算力長約承諾超 8,000 億美元，最遠鎖定至 2045 年  
⚫ 各項固定或保底採購、已簽約未起算之租約及後續長期合約合計超過 8,300 億美元，包括
博通（五年 1,610 億美元租約及後續最高 1,500 億美元支出）、AMD（逾 200 億美
元）、三大雲端巨頭（十年至少 2,694 億美元）等。合約多採能源長約常見的「照付不
議」（用量不足仍須補足差額）條款，成本提前鎖定，而公司 80% 收入卻採依用量實際
結算，形成節奏差異。

### Page 4

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。
7. 每月支付給馬斯克的 SpaceX 算力費高達 31 億美元
⚫ Anthropic 租用 SpaceX.AI（原 xAI）位於孟菲斯的資料中心，合約總額最高達 1,205
億美元，合約至 2029 年。12 月算力全面上線後每月費用約 31 億美元，該月費已接近
SpaceX 今年首季整個 AI 業務營收（8.18 億美元）的四倍之多。
8. 巨頭持股版圖與極短的解禁安排
⚫ 若以 2 兆美元估值計算，亞馬遜全面轉股後約持股近 20%（約 4,000 億美元，超出
12% 上限部分暫以票據保留），Google 持股約 7% 並擁有後續增資與基石投資權限。
解禁期不同於傳統 180 天限制，上市後 76 天內設五個解禁日，首日即允許非創辦人董
事、高管與部分員工減持套現。
9. 特殊雙層治理架構，創辦團隊握有 50.1%絕對投票權
⚫ 普通股東對決策幾乎無控制權，亞馬遜與 Google 持有的 N 類股亦無投票權。公司頂層

由「長期利益信託」（LTBT）掌控制衡，而七位共同創辦人則透過僅 1 股、每人出資 1
美元認購的「F 類股」掌握 50.1% 的絕對投票權，屬於那斯達克規則下的「受控公
司」。
10. 股價每漲 50%，最高提撥 2%股權投入公益
⚫ 上市前已將全面攤薄股本的 5%（約 8.47 億股 N 類股）劃入 Anthropic 公益基金。董
事會批准未來股價若從發行價起每上漲 50%，可再額外注入最高 2% 股份；同時七位共
同創辦人承諾將個人 80% 的股權收益用於公益捐贈。
11. 長達 90 多頁的「生存級」風險因子揭露
⚫ 篇幅約為 SpaceX 的三倍，直接提及技術可能對人類構成「災難性或生存級風險」、可能
被惡意行為者用於開發致命武器，甚至可能衍生出模型抵制關機、操縱資訊或勒索等失控
行為。招股書直言，此類系統性風險恐怕連整個全球保險業都無力承保。
12. 創辦團隊向投資者發出的理念警告
⚫ 七位創辦人公開呼籲投資人切勿純以短期財務交易視角看待公司，直言部分商業決策會在
公共利益與利潤最大化之間權衡，作法可能與股東預期背道而馳。同時，團隊也著重強調

創始成員至今未流失，與競爭對手 OpenAI 形成對比。
13. 上市股票代碼為「ANT」，展現低自我企業文化
⚫ Anthropic 將以股票代碼「ANT」在那斯達克掛牌。招股書以「low-ego」（低自我）形
容內部文化，員工內部自稱為「Ants」（螞蟻），形成文化與代碼的巧妙呼應。

### Page 5

以上資料為元大證券國際金融業務部依上手提供研究報告摘譯，僅供內部教育訓練使用。 
14. 市場下一步檢驗指標：10 月 ARR 表現  
⚫ 市場高度關注 S-1 補充文件中將揭露的 10 月 Run-Rate Revenue（以近 28 天用量年
化加計訂閱費）。目前市場預期 730 億美元為合理基準，若突破 780 億美元，將大幅提
高市場對其在 12 月達成 1,000 億美元 ARR 的信心，成為左右其 1.5 兆至 2 兆美元 
IPO 估值的關鍵密碼。

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
September 14, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.

Officer (CIO).

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

Value-oriented assets over Growth.
Higher real yields in a strong capital
spending environment reinforce this
preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Exhibit 1: Low Yields Post-2008/2009 GFC* Have Fueled a Borrowing Binge.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

2 Source: Bloomberg as of September 9, 2026.

The recent rise in Treasury yields
reflects a collision of fiscal co ncerns,
resilient growth, persistent inflation,
and record debt issuance from both
the Treasury and corporate borrowers.
While this has created periodic b outs
of market pressures, we view the
recent move as a normalization
process, not a cri sis of confidence .

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investmen t Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in
yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to concerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to
other Go

vernment bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason f
or yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still abo
ve average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

Document 328: Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13028742     1

Economy 
Insurance costs hit the brakes 
16 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Property insurance (e.g., auto, homeowners, renters) payment growth has slowed considerably, according to Bank of America 
payments data. Median payments rose just 1% year-over-year (YoY) in 2026, easing further from the 6% growth seen in 2025, 
after a roughly 12% increase in 2024, suggesting that many consumers are getting some relief from rising insurance payments. 
• However, the moderation is uneven across consumers. Bank of America payments data suggests that Gen Z continues to 
experience insurance payment growth of nearly 6%, while increases for other generations are closer to 1% to 2%. Payments 
also rose nearly 1.5% for higher-income households but edged lower for those with lower incomes. 
• Lower accident rates, easing vehicle prices, and softer major-purchase growth may be reducing cost pressures, but geography 
remains a major dividing line. Florida metros recorded declines of roughly 7% to 8%, while California and several Northeastern 
metros continued to see relatively strong increases.

Insurance payments stabilize after years of painful growth 
The burden of higher property (e.g., auto, renters, homeowners) insurance payments may be easing for some. Notably, while the 
average payment increased 3% year-over-year (YoY), the median payment was only up 1%, according to Bank of America 
payments data. This suggests that most consumers have only seen a small increase in their premiums, though there may be a 
few outliers (Exhibit 1).  
The latest data stands in contrast to just two years ago in 2024, when most consumers saw their insurance payments surge 
about 12% YoY, after a nearly 8% YoY increase in 2023.   
Exhibit 1: Insurance payments have increased only modestly through August of this year, with growth peaking in 2024 
Median and average insurance payments (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Insurance payments by age show a similar pattern, with much stronger growth in 2024 across most generations followed by a 
cooling since then (Exhibit 2). Gen Z is the outlier here, with their payments still growing at a healthy rate, increasing almost 6% 
YoY for both 2026 and 2025, after a 7% increase in 2024. Meanwhile, Millennials and Baby Boomers experienced a 2% YoY 
increase so far this year, with Gen X only seeing around a 1% rise.

Bank of America payments data by income provides even more insight. Higher-income households paid nearly 1.5% more in 
2026 than they did a year ago, while middle-income households saw only modest increases or none at all (Exhibit 3). Conversely, 
lower-income households actually paid slightly less so far this year than they did last year.  
Exhibit 3: Higher-income households are still paying more for insurance compared to last year, while lower earners are paying less 
Median insurance payments by income cuts (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Fewer accidents and lower vehicles prices may be driving insurance payments down 
But why might insurance payments be declining at all? One thing to keep in mind is that insurance companies often raise rates 
to cover events that occurred in previous years to recover costs, while adjusting for changing risks.  
For example, the crash rate – the number of vehicle crashes per one million miles driven – suggests that there was a sharp rise 
in accidents in 2021, according to the Department of Transportation (Exhibit 4). Additionally, new and used vehicle prices 
increased significantly after the pandemic, according to Consumer Price Index (CPI) data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics 
(BLS) (Exhibit 5). So, insurers were not only facing more vehicle replacements due to a higher crash rate, but a higher cost to 
replace vehicles as well.

However, the crash rate mostly declined from 2021 to 2024, and the Department of Transportation reported that the number of 
accidents likely decreased again in 2025. Also, while the cost to maintain and repair vehicles has consistently grown since 2021, 
the price to replace vehicles peaked in 2022 and has eased since then – the latter likely a more important factor in insurance 
costs than the former. Additionally, while the CPI index for motor vehicle insurance has fallen 5% YoY, it’s possible that this may 
partially be due to rebates, according to BofA Global Research. So, the price figured into CPI may not be fully realized by the 
consumer.

these major purchases are still K-shaped (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?), it could help to explain the divergence in insurance payment growth across income cohorts.

Florida’s metros have seen the largest decline when compared to other major cities 
Examining different cities across the US also sheds light on insurance trends. Consumers in major California metros saw the 
largest increases in 2026, following near double digit growth in 2025, likely driven by widespread property damage from 
wildfires, according to BofA Global Research (Exhibit 7). Major coastal metros in the Northeast like Boston and New York also 
experienced sizable increases as well.  
Conversely, many cities throughout the Sun Belt like Houston, Nashville and Dallas saw flat or even declining insurance 
payments. BofA Global Research points to several reasons for these declines: part of the equation in this region is that the 
massive runup in the cost of property insurance and domestic migration to the region through 2024 has since eased or reversed 
course.   
Exhibit 7: California major metros have seen steady insurance payment growth, while Miami metros have seen declines 
Median insurance payments by core based statistical areas (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In Florida, legislation that went into effect in 2023, eventually made its way into insurance prices in 2025, leading to widespread 
declines in premiums. The state also has a “lookback period” of three years, where insurance profits can be deemed too high and 
a rebate is issued to consumers. It’s possible this rebate is factoring into declines this year, as some carriers only allow rebates 
upon renewals.  
The Sunshine State was a notable standout for declines, with Orlando, Tampa and Jacksonville each down around 8% YoY in 
2026, followed closely by Miami with a 7% decrease. Additionally, Bank of America payments data suggests that Jacksonville, 
Miami and Orlando all had median insurance payments above the national level in 2023, though their payments were fell below 
that in 2026 (Exhibit 8).  
Major metros in Texas also saw easing insurance payments relative to 2023, while Seattle, San Jose, and Riverside, CA are 
among the major metros that went from below the national median to above in the three-year period ending in 2026.
